      table:speaker spot (src/skin/table/BarnTable.tsx): a spotlight from the rafters on whoever is on turn; no lamp is drawn for it
      farm:door spill (src/skin/table/FarmBackdrop.tsx): the warm light that falls out of the doorway onto the path
      farm:door bounce (src/skin/table/FarmBackdrop.tsx): that light bounced back off the snow, so the hero's face is not a silhouette
      duel:board light (src/skin/table/DuelTable.tsx): a warm light in front of the letter board, whose face the moon does not reach
      duel:speaker spot (src/skin/table/DuelTable.tsx): the hard light from above that finds whoever is on turn
      bedroom:room side (src/skin/dream/BedroomBubble.tsx): the lamp's glow from the room side, so his face reads whichever way he lies
      podium:stage spot (src/skin/table/Podium.tsx): the stage's key light, from over the audience
      podium:front fill (src/skin/table/Podium.tsx): a warm light in front of the podium, on the dancers' faces
      burst flash (src/skin/table/Blast.tsx): the flash of a burst: it is dark between bursts and there is nothing to hang it on
      door bake (src/skin/loading/DoorsRender.tsx): the warm side light of the page that bakes the loading doors (development builds only); the doorway has no lantern
    ### NOT CHECKED: 7 lights whose lamp this checker cannot measure
      dream:sign lantern (src/skin/dream/DreamStage.tsx): it is moved to the lantern of the sign in play, and the signs come and go with the night: they are models, not part of the scene
      podium:hayloft lanterns (src/skin/table/Podium.tsx): its three lanterns are three.js boxes on the hayloft beam, not voxels: the scene checker does not lay them out
      podium:trophy (src/skin/table/Podium.tsx): it rides the winner's trophy, which is a model the winner holds up, not part of the scene
      bomb glow (src/skin/table/Bomb.tsx): it is moved to the bomb every frame; the bomb is a model, not part of a scene
      admin photo glare (src/skin/avatars/AvatarDisc.tsx): it rides the admin's photo disc, the one thing in the game that is not voxels
      leaderboards:trophy (src/skin/boards/BoardStage.tsx): it stands by the trophy on first place's pedestal in the leaderboards' sky, a scene the checker does not build
      barn book:preview (src/skin/shop/PreviewStage.tsx): it lights the bomb on the Barn Book's stand; the preview is not one of the seven scenes

### docs/visual-overhaul/human/m12.md

> # Human checks for the visual overhaul (milestone 12)
> 
> Plan file 16 says milestone 12 is not done until these have answers. Fill in one block per person and device. Nobody
> has filled any in yet (2026-11-17).
> 
> ## 1. The five-minute friends' test
> 
> At least one result from an A1 (school Chromebook) or A2 (Windows laptop with Intel graphics) device. More are better.
> 
> 1. Close other tabs and note the battery. Open the game with `?report=1` at the end of the address, and count the
>    seconds until the barn doors open.
> 2. Press **Copy device report** and paste it below.
> 3. Take a screenshot of the start screen after ten seconds.
> 4. Open Practice, press Start run, type three words, and take a screenshot on your turn.
> 5. Let the bomb go off. Did anything vanish, flicker, turn grey or show a torn-picture icon? One sentence.
> 6. Open the Trading Post, then the Barn Book, press any picture, and take a screenshot.
> 7. After five minutes, note the battery again, and whether the device got hot or loud.
> 
> ```
> Who:
> Device (A1 to A6 or B1 to B6 in file 16, plus its name):
> Browser and version:
> Date:
> Seconds until the doors opened:
> Battery at start / after five minutes:
> Hot or loud?:
> Anything vanish, flicker, turn grey or show a torn-picture icon?:
> Device report:
> Screenshots (attach to the pull request):
> ```
> 
> ## 2. Devices (file 16, section 7)
> 
> A devices must play well; B devices must show a clear message, never a blank page.
> 
> | | Device | Browser | Who | Date | Result |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | A1 | School Chromebook, Celeron N4020 or N4500, 4 GB, 1366 x 768 | Chrome | | | |
> | A2 | Windows laptop, Intel UHD or Iris Xe | Chrome, Edge | | | |
> | A3 | iPhone, three to five years old (low-power mode caps 30 fps) | Safari | | | |
> | A4 | iPad 9th or 10th generation | Safari | | | |
> | A5 | Mid-range Android (Mali or Adreno 6xx) | Chrome | | | |
> | A6 | MacBook, M-series | Chrome, Safari | | | |
> | B1 | iPhone or iPad on iOS 15 | Safari | | | |
> | B2 | Chromebook past its update date (Chrome under 111) | Chrome | | | |
> | B3 | WebGL off or blocked | any | CI fault suite stands in | 2026-11-17 | passes (e2e-painted faults) |
> | B4 | Two-GPU laptop on battery | Chrome | | | |
> | B5 | 120 or 144 Hz screen | Chrome | | | |
> | B6 | Desktop Firefox | Firefox | | | |
>

- fences: src/skin/__tests__/fences.json is new: it pins 290 fenced files and 44 saved keys.
- baked: 3 baked pictures are known to be stale, and counted (baked.knownStale, which may only fall): public/loading/doors-leaves.webp, public/loading/doors-lintel.webp, public/loading/doors-sill.webp. Baked once, on 2026-11-08 (commit 7e951fd), and never since. The next day the rounded voxel corners came in (ROUNDED_VOXELS, commit 4a8765e of 2026-11-09) and src/skin/loading/DoorsRender.tsx changed its shadow type (commit 73677a3), so the picture has sharp corners where the page that bakes it now draws rounded ones. Read from the repo's history in milestone 0; no picture was baked to see how much it shows. Baking them again changes a picture players see, so when to do it is Brian's to say.
- baked: tools/bake/baked.json is new since the base: its 47 entries are first stamps. They record each model and each picture as they were when it was written, and prove nothing about whether a picture matched its model before: no picture was baked again to find out. From here on a model, a page or a tool that changes without its picture being baked again fails npm test or check:scenes by name.
- scenes: EXEMPT.json excuses "buildDistance" in the table scene's connect check with no words from Brian yet (pending). The low hills behind the barn and the far mountain range stand apart from the yard's ground: there is open air between the yard and the hills, and between the far hills and the range. The old tool excused everything built by buildDistance. Plan file 18, section 8, question 1, asks him; until he answers, the pieces it excuses are listed in the report and counted.
- scenes: EXEMPT.json excuses "thing "clock"" in the bedroom scene's connect check with no words from Brian yet (pending). The alarm clock on the nightstand is set down by its mesh, and is not one piece: its face stands one cell in front of its drum, its two feet touch the drum only at a corner, and the ring lines it throws when it goes off hang in the air beside it. The old tool excused every bedroom part placed by a mesh. Plan file 18, section 8, question 1, asks him; until he answers, the pieces it excuses are listed in the report and counted.
- counts: The plan's "renders a second at 144 Hz" (134 today) is a real frame rate, which a held clock and software rendering cannot give: NOT RUN. What is handed in instead is on how many of a faked 144 Hz display's ticks the game drew in one second of the page's clock (144 today: every tick), which is the number milestone 2's cap brings down.
- counts: Oscillators a second in a hidden tab is measured in real time and comes in whole bars of music, so it is not the same three times running: it is in the report's data with its spread and is not a ratchet. When milestone 2 has made it 0 every time, it can be handed in.
- counts: The table's canvas against its box is 1 under reduced motion, which is how every ratchet here is counted. With motion on it follows a CSS animation that runs on real time and is different from run to run: that reading is in the data with its spread and is not a ratchet (milestone 1 fixes the canvas; the reduced-motion ratchet then guards it).
- counts: Every state is counted under reduced motion, as the gate's pictures are taken (the closer look excepted: its 3D only exists with motion on). The worst frame is the worst of one second of a held state, not of a whole session: the figures taken over a whole game are the results state's "worst frame of the whole game" and "once in play", a frame every 250 ms of a game nobody types in.
- counts: Every state is counted at each of the three tiers, pinned by ?gfx=high, medium and low (milestone 4): an unpinned session opens on Medium. "high" is the plan's High, the base's one picture. Medium and low are new names with nothing stored yet.
- counts: A per-second mean (trianglesMean, drawsMean) counts as repeated when its runs are within 0.01 % of it (at least 0.02): which frames fall inside the counted second can move by one. Every other ratchet must be exactly the same in every run.
- Plan file 19, section 2, asks question 13 at this milestone. Each answer is recorded, dated and quoted, in docs/visual-overhaul/human/m12.md.
